qJES: After 2 consecutive iterations without improvement (f_min unchanged at 19.880) and a highly anisotropic landscape (lengthscales std dev=15.091), qJES (Joint Entropy Search) is selected. Unlike previous information-theoretic methods (qMES, qPES) that failed to escape the local minimum, qJES explicitly maximizes the reduction in joint entropy about the global optimum across the entire search space, which is critical for high-dimensional optimization with uncertain model scales (lengthscales std dev=15.091). The increased shortest distance (2.1689) indicates movement away from the previous local minimum, but the flat objective requires a more sophisticated information-driven strategy. qJES efficiently targets regions that simultaneously reduce uncertainty about the global optimum and the function values, making it uniquely suited to break the stagnation cycle while leveraging the remaining 43 iterations.
qMES: After 2 consecutive iterations without improvement (f_min unchanged at 19.880) and a highly anisotropic landscape (lengthscales std dev=61.975), qMES (Max-value Entropy Search) is selected. Unlike previous exploitation-focused methods (PI, PosMean) and exploration-focused methods (PosSTD, LogPI) that failed to escape the plateau, qMES actively targets regions that maximize information gain about the global optimum through an information-theoretic approach. This is crucial in high-dimensional spaces (D=50) with a complex, anisotropic landscape where standard exploration/exploitation balances fail. qMES efficiently balances global search without reckless exploration, making it the only viable option to break the stagnation cycle while leveraging the remaining 42 iterations.
qPES: After 2 consecutive iterations without improvement (f_min unchanged at 19.880) and a highly anisotropic landscape (lengthscales std dev=25.359), qPES (Predictive Entropy Search) is selected. Unlike previous information-theoretic methods (qMES, qJES) that failed to escape the local minimum, qPES explicitly maximizes the expected reduction in predictive entropy about the global optimum, which is critical for high-dimensional spaces with uncertain model scales (lengthscales std dev=25.359). The increased shortest distance (2.1223) indicates movement away from the previous local minimum, but the flat objective requires a more efficient information-driven exploration strategy. qPES avoids the numerical instability of LogEI/LogPI and the over-exploitation risks of PosMean/UCB, making it the only viable option to break the stagnation cycle with 41 iterations remaining.
TS: After 2 consecutive iterations without improvement (f_min unchanged at 19.880) and a highly anisotropic landscape (lengthscales std dev=41.942), Thompson Sampling (TS) is selected as the only acquisition function not used in the last 2 iterations. Its sampling-based exploration strategy targets high-uncertainty regions (critical for the model's high lengthscales std dev=41.942) without relying on the current best value, breaking the stagnation cycle. The moderate outputscale (0.5106) and high dimensionality (D=50) make TS uniquely suited to escape the local plateau while avoiding reusing failed methods (qPES, qMES) from the last 2 iterations.

Unlike previous information-theoretic methods (qMES, qPES) that failed to escape the local minimum, qJES explicitly maximizes the reduction in joint entropy about the global optimum across the entire search space, which is critical for high-dimensional optimization with uncertain model scales (lengthscales std dev=33.869). The increased shortest distance (1.9763) indicates movement away from the previous local minimum, but the flat objective requires a more sophisticated information-driven strategy. qJES efficiently targets regions that simultaneously reduce uncertainty about the global optimum and the function values, making it uniquely suited to break the stagnation cycle while leveraging the remaining 38 iterations.

Based on the optimization process provided, I can make an educated guess about the function being optimized.
The key characteristics of the optimization process:
- High dimensionality (D=50)
- High anisotropy (lengthscales std dev ranging from 4.579 to 14.157)
- The objective function value has remained constant at 19.592 for the last 10 iterations (N=191 to N=200)
- The shortest distance fluctuated between 1.055 and 2.287, indicating exploration around the current best point
- The optimization has been using TS (Thompson Sampling) and EI (Expected Improvement) as acquisition functions
Given these characteristics, the function being optimized is most likely a **scaled and shifted Rastrigin function**.
The Rastrigin function is a common benchmark for high-dimensional optimization with many local minima. Its standard form has a global minimum of 0, but it's often scaled and shifted for testing purposes. The Rastrigin function is defined as:
f(x) = A * n + Σ [x_i^2 - A * cos(2πx_i)]
Where A is a constant (usually 10), and n is the dimensionality.
In this case, the function appears to be a scaled and shifted version where the minimum value is 19.592 instead of 0. This would explain why the optimization has been stuck at this value for many iterations - it's likely the global minimum of the function being optimized.
The high anisotropy (lengthscales std dev up to 14.157) is also characteristic of the Rastrigin function, which has a complex landscape with varying smoothness across dimensions.
So my guess is that the function being optimized is a scaled and shifted Rastrigin function.
